allows for more flexibility than conditioning on the first steps of
M
, as it allows to condition on partial information about one of the jumps. In particular, this “spatial” Markov property explains why we can expect independence across scales, even though one single step of
M
can in theory jump over several scales (see the “spatial” filtration introduced in (
6.29
)). However, we will not explicitly use this point process representation.

6.3
The coupling argument

Let
M
and
M^{\prime}
be two versions of the decreasing Markov chain introduced in (
6.9
), started from two large values
k
and
k^{\prime}
.
The goal of this section is to construct a coupled version of those chains (see
6.5
) such that, with high probability as
k
and
k^{\prime}
tend to infinity, the last finite number of steps of the two chains before extinction are the same (
6.6
). We will then quickly explain how to upgrade this coupling to the full Markov chain
Z^{k}
(
6.7
), and how to deduce Theorem
6.2
.
The bulk of this section is devoted to the proof of
6.6
, which will be completed in
Section

6.3.5
, building on the coupling estimates of
Section

6.3.4
.

6.3.1
Introducing the coupling: the black and golden instructions

We assume for the moment that there exists an infinite deterministic set of good scales, denoted by
\mathrm{GoodScales}\subset\mathbb{N}
, that satisfy certain regularity conditions; see
6.10
for a precise definition and
6.9
for the existence of such good scales. In particular, these good scales are
well-separated
, in the sense that if
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
with
\ell^{\prime}>\ell
then
\ell^{\prime}>7\ell
.

Then in
6.5
, we construct a coupling
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}
of the two Markov chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
in (
6.9
), with marginals
\mathbb{P}_{k}
and
\mathbb{P}_{k^{\prime}}
respectively,
by using a set of rules for determining what step the chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
take at every site of
\mathbb{N}
. The sort of coupling that we have in mind is what Aldous and Thorisson coined as
shift-couplings

[
AT93
]
, since the two chains will
a priori
merge at different times in their own evolutions.

First, we introduce the
black instructions

(B_{m})_{m\geq 1}
as independent random variables defined so that

\displaystyle\forall m^{\prime}<m,\qquad\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}=m^{\prime}}}\right)=\pi(m;m^{\prime}),

(6.16)

where
\pi(m;m^{\prime})
is as in (
6.2.2
).
Then, for every good scale
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
, we introduce the
golden instructions

(G_{m})_{5\ell\leq m\leq 7\ell}
that are coupled together for a given good scale
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
, but independent between different good scales and independent of the black instructions
(B_{m})_{m\geq 1}
, with marginal law

\displaystyle\forall m^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\quad\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{m}=m^{\prime}}}\right)=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}=m^{\prime}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}B_{m}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right).

(6.17)

The coupling of
(G_{m})_{5\ell\leq m\leq 7\ell}
will be chosen so that, with uniformly positive probability over all
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
, we have

\forall a,a^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\qquad G_{a}=G_{a^{\prime}}.

The existence of such coupling of the golden instructions will be shown later in
Item

3
of
6.14
.

Definition 6.5

(The black-golden coupling)
.

We say that the decreasing chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
have the
black-golden coupling
when the dynamics of
M
(and
M^{\prime}
) are described for all
t\geq 0
as follows:
14
14

14

We stress that here we are using a
unique
set of black and golden instructions for
both
chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
.

M_{t+1}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} &G_{M_{t}}\qquad\text{if }M_{t}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\text{ for some good scale $\ell$ and }B_{M_{t}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket};\\
&B_{M_{t}}\qquad\text{otherwise.}\end{aligned}}}\right.

(6.18)

Moreover, when two chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
are started from
k
and
k^{\prime}
respectively, we denote their joint law under this coupling by
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}
.

In more intuitive terms, the chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
typically follow the black instructions, except in a specific situation: when a chain is instructed to jump – via a black instruction – from a position in
\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
to somewhere inside
\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
, it then follows the corresponding golden instruction instead.
It is clear from this definition that the marginals
M
and
M^{\prime}
under
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}
are indeed Markov chains with transition probabilities
\pi
as in (
6.2.2
).

For all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the black instruction
B_{m}
should be thought of as the instruction to take a
small step
from
m
to
B_{m}
, while for all
m\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
the golden instruction
G_{m}
should be thought of as the instruction to take a
large step
from
m\geq 5\ell
to
G_{m}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
. The reason why we consider golden instructions pointing towards
\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
rather than
\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
is that we will run a multiscale argument that requires the chains not to jump across several good scales at once. Furthermore, the reason for the terminology for colors is that when taking a golden instruction, the chains will merge with positive probability and then stick together,
15
15

15

We emphasize that this is not the only mechanism by which the two chains can merge. In fact, it is totally possible for them to merge through two black instructions that happen to land at the same value, causing the chains to stick together from that point onward. However, since our aim is to lower bound the probability that the two chains will merge fast enough, it suffices for us to focus on the case involving the golden instructions.
ending up in a win, as precisely described in the next result.

We will write
V_{\mer}
for the merging value of
M
and
M^{\prime}
,
i.e.
the largest common value taken by the chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
. Let also
\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}
(resp.
\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}
) be the time when
M
(resp.
M^{\prime}
) visits
V_{\mer}
. Note that by definition of the black-golden coupling,

(M_{\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}+s})_{s\geq 0}=(M^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}+s})_{s\geq 0}.

Finally, for
k\geq A\geq 1
, we denote by
s(A,k)
the number of good scales
\ell\in\mathopen{\llbracket}A\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},k\mathclose{\rrbracket}
such that
7\ell<k
.

Proposition 6.6

(Fast merging of
M
and
M^{\prime}
)
.

Let
M
and
M^{\prime}
be defined as the decreasing chains started from
k
and
k^{\prime}
, respectively, under the black-golden coupling
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}
of
6.5
.
Then, the following assertions hold:

1.

There exists a constant
c\in(0,1)
such that for all
k^{\prime}\geq k\geq 1
and all
A\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{\mer}<A}}\right)\leq(1-c)^{s(A,k)}.

2.

Furthermore, the total times
\mathtt{T}-\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}
and
\mathtt{T}^{\prime}-\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}
, representing the durations between merging and dying, tend to infinity in probability as
k,k^{\prime}\to\infty
.

The proof of
6.6
will occupy most of the remainder of
Section

6
. Once this is proved, we easily obtain a similar result for the whole Markov chain
Z^{k}
. We note that with the notation of (
6.8
), the time where
M^{*}
(resp.
(M^{*})^{\prime}
) first hits
V_{\mer}
is given by
\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}
(resp.
\mathchoice{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}
). We also recall that
\eta=\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{T}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{T}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{T}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{T}$}}}
(resp.
\eta^{\prime}=\mathchoice{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{T}^{\prime}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathtt{T}^{\prime}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{T}^{\prime}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathtt{T}^{\prime}$}}}
) is the hitting time of
0
by
M^{*}
(resp.
(M^{*})^{\prime}
).

Corollary 6.7
.

For any
k^{\prime}\geq k\geq 1
, there exists a coupling
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k,k^{\prime}}
of the full Markov chains
Z^{k}
and
Z^{k^{\prime}}
started from
k
and
k^{\prime}
respectively, under which:

•

The pair
(M,M^{\prime})
follows the black-golden coupling
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}
of
6.5
.

•

For any
h\geq 1
, we have
Z^{k}_{\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}+h}={Z}^{k^{\prime}}_{\mathchoice{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}+h}
.

In particular:

1.

For all
A\geq 1
, we have

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{\mer}<A}}\right)\leq(1-c)^{s(A,k)}.

2.

For all
A\geq 1
, we have

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{\mer}\geq A}}\right)\to 1\quad\text{as }k,k^{\prime}\to\infty.

3.

The durations
\eta-\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}
and
\eta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}
go to infinity in probability as
k,k^{\prime}\to\infty
.

Proof of Corollary
6.7
given Proposition
6.6
.

It is explained in Section
6.2.2
how to reconstruct
Z^{k}
from
M
using independent decorations
\eta,S,D,W
, and similarly for
Z^{k^{\prime}}
. Since
M
and
M^{\prime}
merge at respective times
\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}
and
\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}
, we can choose these decorations such that for all
t\geq 0
and all
h\geq 1
,

\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}+t+1$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}+t+1$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}+t+1$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}+t+1$}}}-\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}+t$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}+t$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}+t$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}+t$}}}=\mathchoice{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}+t+1$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}+t+1$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}+t+1$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}+t+1$}}}-\mathchoice{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}+t$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}+t$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}+t$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}+t$}}}\quad\text{and}\quad(S,D,W)_{\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}+h}=(S^{\prime},D^{\prime},W^{\prime})_{\mathchoice{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.64583pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}+h}.

This implies that
Z^{k}
after time
\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}+1
and
Z^{k^{\prime}}
after time
\mathchoice{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}+1
coincide, which shows the existence of
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k,k^{\prime}}
.
Item

1
then follows immediately from
Item

1
of Proposition
6.6
, and implies
Item

2
because our set of good scales is infinite. Finally,
Item

3
follows from
Item

2
of Proposition
6.6
since
\eta-\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}\geq\mathtt{T}-\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}
and
\eta^{\prime}-\mathchoice{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}\geq\mathtt{T}^{\prime}-\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.2
given Corollary
6.7
.

We need to prove that there exists a process
(\overset{\leftarrow}{Z}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
such that, for all
r\geq 1
,

d_{\mathrm{TV}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((Z^{k}_{\eta-t})_{t\leq r},(\overset{\leftarrow}{Z}_{{t}})_{t\leq r}}}\right)\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as }k\to\infty,

(6.19)

where we take the convention that
Z^{k}_{\eta-t}=(\ominus,{\raisebox{1.5pt}{\smash{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{357266}{131072}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\leftarrow$}}}}}}},k,0)
when
t>\eta
.
We prove (
6.19
) by Cauchy characterization. Consider the two decreasing Markov chains
Z^{k}
and
Z^{k^{\prime}}
started at
k
and
k^{\prime}
respectively and having the coupling
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k,k^{\prime}}
of Corollary
6.7
.
By standard coupling techniques (see e.g.
[
LP17
, Proposition 4.7]
),
we can write

d_{\mathrm{TV}}\Big((Z^{k}_{\eta-{t}})_{t\leq r},({Z}^{k^{\prime}}_{\eta^{\prime}-{t}})_{t\leq r}\Big)\leq\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Z^{k}_{\eta-{t}}\neq{Z}^{k^{\prime}}_{\eta^{\prime}-{t}}\text{ for some $t\leq r$}}}\right)\leq\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\eta-\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}\leq r}}\right)

since, under the coupling, the two chains
Z^{k}
and
Z^{k^{\prime}}
stick together after
\mathchoice{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}_{\mer}$}}}
and
\mathchoice{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}{\eta^{\prime}_{\lower 0.90417pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{\mer}$}}}
.
Then,
Item

3
of
6.7
guarantees that the probability on the right-hand side of the last display goes to
0
as
k,k^{\prime}\to\infty
, which proves the desired Cauchy characterization.

We conclude that for any fixed
r
, the random vector
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(Z^{k}_{\eta-t}}}\right)_{t\leq r}
started from
k
has a limit as
k\to\infty
that we write
\big(\overset{\leftarrow}{Z}_{t}\big)_{t\leq r}
.
Finally, an application of Kolmogorov’s extension theorem shows that the limit defines a valid stochastic process
\overset{\leftarrow}{Z}
.
∎

All we have left to do to prove
6.2
is therefore to prove
6.6
, which will be completed in
Section

6.3.5
. As it forms the most technical part of the paper, the reader may on a first read directly jump to
Section

7
.

The rest of this section is organized as follows: in
Section

6.3.2
we will give an informal description of the strategy for the proof of
6.6
. Then,
Section

6.3.3
is devoted to the description of the good scales,
Section

6.3.4
to the proof of the main technical estimates, and finally
Section

6.3.5
to the conclusion of the proof of
6.6
.

6.3.2
Description of the strategy for the coupling argument

We now describe the strategy for the proof of Item
1
of
6.6
; the second item will follow as a simple consequence.
We will show that there exists a constant
c\in(0,1)
such that:

1.

From any starting point
k
, for each good scale
\ell
such that
7\ell\leq k
, the chain
M
visits
\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
with probability at least
c
(this will be a consequence of
Item

1
in
6.14
).

2.

If
M
is ever in
\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
, it will try to jump below
4\ell
from somewhere in
\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
with probability at least
c
, meaning it will use a golden instruction with probability at least
c
(this is a consequence of
Item

2
in
6.14
). Moreover, conditional on this, the same facts hold for
M^{\prime}
with probability at least
c/2
(this is a consequence of
6.15
).

3.

For all good scales
\ell
, we can couple the
(G_{m})_{5\ell\leq m\leq 7\ell}
so that with probability at least
c
they are all equal (this is
Item

3
in
6.14
).

4.

Since, for any good scale,
Item

1
in
6.14
holds conditionally on all the information above
7\ell
, we can iterate the argument at each scale.

We recall from the discussion around (
6.2.2
) that
M
can be compared to a decreasing random walk with jump distribution
(q(i))_{i\geq 1}
, with
q(i)=i^{-\gamma+o(1)}
as
i\to\infty
, and
\gamma\in(3/2,2)
as in (
2.7
). Informally speaking, the
good scales
that we need in Steps
1
,
2
and
3
are scales where the behavior of this walk is reasonably close to that of a stable subordinator.
16
16

16

The convergence of such a walk to a
(\gamma-1)
-stable subordinator would be immediate given
7.1
, but unfortunately proving
7.1
is precisely our long-term goal.

If all the above is true (and using the fact that
M
and
M^{\prime}
are essentially independent up until merging),
there is a uniformly positive probability at every good scale that both
M
and
M^{\prime}
follow two golden instructions landing at the same height.
Since we can iterate this attempt to land at the same height by successive conditionings above each good scale (thanks to Step
4
above), with probability tending to one as the starting points approach infinity, the two chains will eventually follow two golden instructions landing at the same height. From this, we can conclude by construction of the black-golden coupling that the future trajectories of
M
and
M^{\prime}
will stick together beyond this point, completing the proof of
Item

1
of
6.6
.

6.3.3
Definition and existence of good scales and additional preliminary estimates

In this whole section, the value of the constants
c
and
C
may change from line to line.
Recall that, in
Section

5
, we proved the rough regularity estimates for the sequences
q
and
Q
stated in
5.1
, which have the following immediate consequences.

Corollary 6.8

(Bounds on
q
and
Q
)
.

Fix
r,R>0
. Then there exist constants
c,C>0
such that:

1.

For all
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle c\leq\frac{kq(k)}{Q(k)}\leq C.

(6.20)

2.

For all
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
,

\frac{q(k^{\prime})}{q(k)}\geq c.

(6.21)

3.

For all
k,k^{\prime}\geq 1
with
r\leq\frac{k^{\prime}}{k}\leq R
,

\displaystyle c\leq\frac{q(k^{\prime})}{q(k)}\leq C\qquad\text{and}\qquad c\leq\frac{Q(k^{\prime})}{Q(k)}\leq C.

(6.22)

Proof.

The bounds in (
6.20
) are exactly the ones in
Equation

5.9
in
5.3
.
The upper bound in (
5.2
) of
5.1
gives that
\frac{q(k)}{q(k^{\prime})}\leq C
for all
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
, which immediately implies (
6.21
).
Finally,
5.1
ensures that as long as the ratio
\frac{k^{\prime}}{k}
is bounded above and below by some fixed constants,
so are the ratios
\frac{q(k^{\prime})}{q(k)}
and
\frac{Q(k^{\prime})}{Q(k)}
. This gives the
bounds in (
6.22
).
∎

In order to show the existence of large jumps, we will need an additional regularity result holding (at least) for an infinite number of integers
\ell
.

Lemma 6.9

(Good scale condition)
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that there are infinitely many integers
\ell\geq 1
satisfying the inequality

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}iq(i)\leq C\ell Q(\ell).

(6.23)

Proof.

We prove that there exists a constant
c>0
such that for infinitely many values of
\ell
we have

\ell Q(\ell)\geq c\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}Q(i).

(6.24)

By the bounds in (
6.20
), this would imply our claim.
For all
\ell\geq 1
, we write
\mathbf{Q}(\ell)=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}Q(i)
.
By
5.1
, we know that
Q(i)=i^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}+o(1)}
where
\alpha\in(1/2,1)
.
This entails that

\mathbf{Q}(\ell)=\ell^{1-\frac{1}{2\alpha}+o(1)}.

(6.25)

Now, assume for a contradiction that for any choice of the constant
c>0
, (
6.24
) fails for all large enough
\ell
.
Then, for all large enough
\ell
, we have
\mathbf{Q}(\ell+1)-\mathbf{Q}(\ell)=Q(\ell)<\frac{c}{\ell}\mathbf{Q}(\ell)
, so that
\mathbf{Q}(\ell+1)<\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{c}{\ell}}}\right)\mathbf{Q}(\ell)
. Hence, for all
\ell
large enough,

\mathbf{Q}(\ell)\leq\ell^{c+o(1)}.

We get a contradiction with (
6.25
) if

c<1-\frac{1}{2\alpha}
. Since
1-\frac{1}{2\alpha}>0
as
\alpha\in(1/2,1)
, we conclude that there exists a constant
c>0
such that (
6.24
) holds for infinitely many values of
\ell
.
∎

We can now introduce the good scales that we mentioned at the beginning of
Section

6.3.1
.

Definition 6.10

(Good scales)
.

Fix
C>0
as in
6.9
. We say that an infinite set
\mathrm{GoodScales}\subset\mathbb{N}
is a collection of
good scales
if the following two conditions hold:

1.

Every
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
satisfies the condition
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}iq(i)\leq C\ell Q(\ell)
.

2.

For all
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
with
\ell^{\prime}>\ell
, we have that
\ell^{\prime}>7\ell
.

If
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
, we say that the interval
\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
is a
good interval
.

6.9
guarantees the existence of these collections of good scales and from now on we fix
\mathrm{GoodScales}
to be one arbitrary, but fixed, such collection. Note that good intervals are disjoint by the second condition in the definition.

Remark 6.11
.

Note that, assuming the good regularity estimates in
7.1
, there exists
C>0
such that any integer
\ell\geq 1
satisfies the good scale condition in
Item

1
of
6.10
. Indeed, if
q(i)=i^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1}\varphi(i)
as in
7.1
, then by Karamata’s Theorem (see
[
BGT89
, Theorem 1.5.11]
), we have
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}iq(i)\sim\frac{\ell^{2}q(\ell)}{1-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}
as
\ell\to\infty
, and
\ell^{2}q(\ell)\leq C\ell Q(\ell)
by the bounds in (
6.20
).
Therefore, provided we knew
7.1
, we could take
\mathrm{GoodScales}
to be any collection of well-separated integers. This will be important later for the scaling limit result in
Section

9
.

The bounds in
6.8
and
6.9
help us control the transition probabilities
\pi(m;m^{\prime})
from (
6.2.2
) of the decreasing Markov chain
M
, as shown in the next lemma.

Lemma 6.12

(Bounds for the transition probabilities of
M
)
.

Fix two constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
with
c_{1}<c_{2}
. Then there exist
c>0
and
C>0
such that the following is true:

1.

If
c_{1}<1
, then for all
j\geq 1
,

\forall i<j,\quad\pi(j;i)\geq c\frac{i}{j}q(i)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\forall i\leq c_{1}j,\quad\pi(j;i)\leq C\frac{i}{j}q(i).

2.

For all
\ell\geq 1
, and all
j\geq 1
and
x,y\geq 1
such that
c_{1}\ell\leq x\leq y\leq c_{2}\ell<j
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{t+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}x\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},y\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{t}=j}}\right)\geq c\frac{y-x}{j}Q(\ell).

3.

If
c_{1}<1
, then for all
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
, and all
j\geq 1
and
y\geq 1
such that
y\leq(c_{1}j)\wedge(c_{2}\ell)
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{t+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},y\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{t}=j}}\right)\leq C\frac{\ell}{j}Q(\ell).

4.

If
c_{1}>1
, then for all
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
and
j\in\mathopen{\llbracket}c_{1}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},c_{2}\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
, writing
\Delta M_{1}=M_{0}-M_{1}
,

\mathbb{E}_{j}\big[\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{1}\leq\ell}\big]\leq C\ell Q(\ell).

Proof.

The bounds in
Item

1
follow from
6.8
. Indeed, by (
6.2.2
), we have

\forall i<j,\quad\pi(j;i)=\frac{1}{1+\frac{(1-p)}{p}(Q(j)-\frac{q(j)}{2})}\cdot\frac{i}{j}\cdot\frac{q(i)q(j-i)}{q(j)}.

(6.26)

Now for all
j\geq 1
, the ratio
\frac{1}{1+\frac{(1-p)}{p}(Q(j)-\frac{q(j)}{2})}
is bounded above and below by constants.
Besides, on the one hand, by (
6.21
),

\forall i\leq j,\qquad\frac{q(j-i)}{q(j)}\geq c.

On the other hand, by the first item of (
6.22
), if
c_{1}<1
,

\forall i\leq c_{1}j,\qquad\frac{q(j-i)}{q(j)}\leq C.

since

1-c_{1}\leq 1-\frac{i}{j}=\frac{j-i}{j}\leq 1
.
The bounds in
Item

1
immediately follow.

We now move on to
Item

2
. We recall from
Section

3.1
the notation for sums between real numbers, which will be extensively used in the rest of the proof. Let
\ell\geq 1
, and
j\geq 1
and
x,y\geq 1
, such that
c_{1}\ell\leq x\leq y\leq c_{2}\ell<j
.
Allowing the value of the constant
c
to change from line to line, we get by
Item

1
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{t+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}x\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},y\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{t}=j}}\right)=\sum_{i=x}^{y}\pi(j;i)

\displaystyle\geq c\sum_{i=x}^{y}\frac{i}{j}\cdot q(i)

\displaystyle\geq c\cdot\frac{\ell}{j}\sum_{i=x}^{y}q(i)\geq c\frac{\ell}{j}(y-x)q(\ell),

(6.27)

where in the last inequality we used the first bound in (
6.22
), which holds since we are summing over
i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}x\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},y\mathclose{\rrbracket}\subseteq\mathopen{\llbracket}c_{1}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},c_{2}\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
. By the bounds in (
6.20
), this establishes
Item

2
.

We now assume that
c_{1}<1
and prove
Item

3
. Fix
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
and consider
j\geq 1
and
y\geq 1
such that
y\leq(c_{1}j)\wedge(c_{2}\ell)
. Using the fact that we sum over
i\leq y\leq c_{1}j
, together with the second bound in
Item

1
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{t+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},y\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{t}=j}}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{y}\pi(j;i)\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{y}\frac{i}{j}q(i)\leq\frac{C}{j}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}iq(i)+\sum_{i=\ell}^{y}iq(i)\bigg).

Since
y\leq c_{2}\ell
, the latter sum can be handled again using first the bounds in (
6.22
) and then in (
6.20
), yielding

\sum_{i=\ell}^{y}iq(i)\leq C\ell^{2}q(\ell)\leq C\ell Q(\ell).

The first one is also bounded by
C\ell Q(\ell)
by the good scale condition of
6.10
, which proves
Item

3
.

It only remains to prove
Item

4
for which we assume that
c_{1}>1
. Taking
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
and
j\in\mathopen{\llbracket}c_{1}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},c_{2}\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{j}\big[\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{1}\leq\ell}\big]=\sum_{i=j-\ell}^{j-1}(j-i)\pi(j;i).

Any
i
in this sum satisfies
(c_{1}-1)\ell\leq i<j\leq c_{2}\ell
(with
c_{1}-1>0
). Therefore, we have

\pi(j;i)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:pi(j,i)}}}{{\leq}}C\frac{i}{j}\cdot\frac{q(i)q(j-i)}{q(j)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eqn:regularity_q-part3}}}{{\leq}}Cq(j-i).

Recalling that
\ell
is a good scale, we finally get by the good scale condition (
6.10
):

\mathbb{E}_{j}\big[\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{1}\leq\ell}\big]\leq C\sum_{i=j-\ell}^{j-1}(j-i)q(j-i)=C\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}iq(i)\leq C\ell Q(\ell).

This concludes the proof of
Item

4
and therefore establishes
6.12
.
∎

6.3.4
Statements and proofs of the three main ingredients for the coupling

In this section, we consider two chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
under the black-golden coupling (see
6.5
). Recall the notation
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}
for the probability measure of this coupling under which
M
starts at
k
and
M^{\prime}
at
k^{\prime}
.
Whenever we work with events that only depend on
M
, we use
\mathbb{P}_{k}
for shorter expressions.

We write
H_{i}
for the first hitting time of
\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},i\mathclose{\rrbracket}
by the chain
M
, that is

H_{i}\coloneqq\inf\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{t\geq 1:M_{t}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},i\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right\}.

(6.28)

We define
H_{i}^{\prime}
similarly for
M^{\prime}
.
We will often write
M(H_{i})
instead of
M_{H_{i}}
to avoid notational clutter.
Note that for any
h\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},k\mathclose{\rrbracket}
we have

\displaystyle M(H_{h})

\displaystyle=\sup\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\{M_{t},\ t\geq 0\}\cap\mathopen{[}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},h\mathclose{]}}}\right),

\displaystyle M(H_{h}-1)

\displaystyle=\inf\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\{M_{t},\ t\geq 0\}\cap\mathopen{[}h+1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},\infty\mathclose{)}}}\right).

Define for all
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
the
\sigma
-algebra (
cf.

Remark

6.4
)

\mathcal{G}_{\ell}\coloneqq\sigma\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{m}:m\in\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}>\ell}\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell^{\prime}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell^{\prime}\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\vee\sigma\Bigg(\mathopen{[}7\ell+1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},+\infty\mathclose{)}\cap\{M_{t},t\geq 0\},\mathopen{[}7\ell+1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},+\infty\mathclose{)}\cap\{M_{t}^{\prime},t\geq 0\}\Bigg).

(6.29)

This
\sigma
-algebra records the values of the golden instructions for all golden intervals strictly above
7\ell
, as well as the trajectories of
M
and
M^{\prime}
from their starting point up to the last time that they take a value strictly larger than
7\ell
,
i.e.

(M_{t})_{0\leq t\leq H_{7\ell}-1}
and
(M^{\prime}_{t})_{0\leq t\leq H^{\prime}_{7\ell}-1}
.

Lemma 6.13

(Spatial Markov property at level
7\ell
)
.

Fix
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
and
k,k^{\prime}\geq 7\ell+1
. Let
M
and
M^{\prime}
be defined under the black-golden coupling
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}
of
6.5
. Then the following assertions are true:

1.

For
m\in\mathopen{\llbracket}7\ell+1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},k\mathclose{\rrbracket}
and
m^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}7\ell+1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},k^{\prime}\mathclose{\rrbracket}
, the
\mathcal{G}_{\ell}
-conditional distribution of
(M(H_{7\ell}),M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell}))
on the event
\{M(H_{7\ell}-1)=m,M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell}-1)=m^{\prime}\}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{Law}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((M(H_{7\ell}),M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell}))\ |\ \mathcal{G}_{\ell}}}\right)=\mathrm{Law}((B_{m},B_{m^{\prime}})\ |\ B_{m}\leq 7\ell,B^{\prime}_{m}\leq 7\ell).

(6.30)

2.

Conditionally on
\sigma(\mathcal{G}_{\ell},(M(H_{7\ell}),M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell}))
, the chains
(M(H_{7\ell}+t))_{t\geq 0}
and
(M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell}+t))_{t\geq 0}
have the distribution of
M
and
M^{\prime}
under the black-golden coupling
\mathbb{P}_{M(H_{7\ell}),M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell})}
.

Proof.

We first prove
Item

1
.
Fix any
m,m^{\prime}\geq 7\ell+1
and let us reason on the event
\{M(H_{7\ell}-1)=m,M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell}-1)=m^{\prime}\}
.
We know by definition of
H_{7\ell}
and
H^{\prime}_{7\ell}
that

M(H_{7\ell})\leq 7\ell
and
M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell})\leq 7\ell
.
From the definition of the black-golden coupling, it is clear that we have

M(H_{7\ell})=B_{m}
, because a golden instruction
G_{m}

(if taken at all)
would land in some interval
\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell^{\prime}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell^{\prime}\mathclose{\rrbracket}
with
\ell^{\prime}>7\ell
, which would contradict the fact that
M(H_{7\ell})\leq 7\ell
.
We similarly have
M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell})=B^{\prime}_{m}
.
Now we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Law}((M(H_{7\ell}),M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell}))\ |\ \mathcal{G}_{\ell})=\mathrm{Law}((B_{m},B_{m^{\prime}})\ |\ \mathcal{G}_{\ell})=\mathrm{Law}((B_{m},B_{m^{\prime}})\ |\ B_{m}\leq 7\ell,B^{\prime}_{m}\leq 7\ell),

which proves
Item

1
.

We now turn to
Item

2
.
Just note that by definition of the black-golden coupling, the future trajectories
(M(H_{7\ell}+t))_{t\geq 0}
and
(M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell}+t))_{t\geq 0}
are entirely determined by
the positions
(M(H_{7\ell}),M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell}))

and
the values of the black and golden instructions below
7\ell
, namely
(B_{m})_{1\leq m\leq 7\ell}
and
(G_{m})_{5\ell^{\prime}\leq m\leq 7\ell^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell}
.
Note also that these random variables are independent of

\mathcal{G}_{\ell},M(H_{7\ell}),M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell})
, because those are determined from the value of the black and golden instructions
above

7\ell+1
.
This ensures that
Item

2
holds.
∎

The next lemma presents the three main ingredients for the proof of
6.6
, following the strategy outlined in
Section

6.3.2
. Then, in
6.15
, we will extend the second item to incorporate the coupling with
M^{\prime}
.

Lemma 6.14
.

There exists a constant
c\in(0,1)
such that the following three properties hold:

1.

For any
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
and
m>7\ell
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}B_{m}\leq 7\ell}}\right)\geq c.

2.

For any
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
and
k\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M(H_{4\ell}-1)\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\,,\,M(H_{4\ell})\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\geq c.

3.

For any
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
, we can couple the golden instructions
(G_{m})_{5\ell\leq m\leq 7\ell}
, with law given by (
6.17
), in such a way that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\forall a,a^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\ G_{a}=G_{a^{\prime}}}}\right)\geq c.

See
Figure

11
for a schematic representation of the quantities involved in
6.14
and some further explanations on the role of the three items.

Figure 11:
A schematic representation for the statement of
6.14
. A good scale
\ell
is shown as a dashed-dotted red line and the corresponding value of
7\ell
is shown as a dashed-dotted blue line. The good interval
\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
is divided into six sub-intervals by the dashed black lines, with the sub-intervals
\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
,
\mathopen{\llbracket}4\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},5\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
and
\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
highlighted in blue, brown and gold, respectively.
Items

1
and
2
ensure that there is a uniformly positive probability among all good scales
\ell
that
M
enters the blue sub-interval and then (possibly after a few steps) jumps across the brown sub-interval; this trajectory is schematically represented by the black dots. The jump across the brown good sub-interval corresponds to a golden instruction landing at the golden dot at height
G_{M(H_{4\ell}-1)}
inside the golden interval
\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
. Since
Item

3
allows us to couple all the golden instructions in such a way that they land at
the same
place with positive probability, this (with the additional help of
6.15
) will allow us to argue that there is a uniformly positive probability over all good scales
\ell
that
M
and
M^{\prime}
meet. Since
Item

1
guarantees that this actually holds conditionally on the scales above, we will be able to conclude that
M
and
M^{\prime}
meet with probability tending to one as their starting points tend to infinity.

We prove the three items of
6.14
sequentially.

Proof of
Item

1
of
6.14
.

Let
\ell
be a good scale and
m>7\ell
.
We write

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}B_{m}\leq 7\ell}}\right)=\frac{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}\leq 7\ell}}\right)}=\frac{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{t+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{t}=m}}\right)}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{t+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{t}=m}}\right)},

(6.31)

for any
t\geq 0
.
Now by Item
2
in
6.12
(with
c_{1}=6
,
c_{2}=7
,
x=6\ell
and
y=7\ell
) there is a constant
c>0
(independent of
\ell
) such that for all
m>7\ell
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{t+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{t}=m}}\right)\geq c\frac{\ell}{m}Q(\ell).

Likewise, by Item
3
in
6.12
(with
c_{1}=6/7
,
c_{2}=6
, and
y=6\ell-1
), there is a constant
C>0
(independent of
\ell
) such that for all
m>7\ell
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{t+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},6\ell-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{t}=m}}\right)\leq C\frac{\ell}{m}Q(\ell).

Combining the previous two displays, we find that for all
m>7\ell
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}B_{m}\leq 7\ell}}\right)\geq\frac{c}{C+c}.

This proves
Item

1
of
6.14
.
∎

Proof of
Item

2
of
6.14
.

We divide the proof into five steps.

Step 1
: Introducing a good event
E_{\ell}
.

For
a>0
and
\ell\geq 1
, let
b=b(\ell)\coloneqq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lceil\frac{a}{Q(\ell)}}}\right\rceil,
where
Q(\cdot)
is as in (
2.5
). Define the following event
E_{\ell}
:

(i)

M
takes at most
b
jumps smaller than
\ell
, and then a big jump of size between
3\ell
and
4\ell
.

(ii)

The sum of the above small jumps is actually smaller than
\ell
.

Note that the event
E_{\ell}
depends implicitly on
b
, and therefore on
a
. If the chain
M
starts from
k\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
and satisfies (i) and (ii) above, it first travels less than distance
\ell
in less than
b
units of time (so it stays in
\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
), and then lands in
\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
by a big jump, jumping over
\mathopen{\llbracket}4\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},5\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
. Therefore,

\forall k\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\quad\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M(H_{4\ell}-1)\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},M(H_{4\ell})\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(E_{\ell}}}\right).

(6.32)

We will prove in the next steps that we can find
a>0
such that for some constant
c>0
and all
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
,

\forall k\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\quad\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(E_{\ell}}}\right)\geq c.

(6.33)

Assuming (
6.33
), we note that (
6.32
) yields the claim in
Item

2
of
6.14
. It remains to prove (
6.33
).

Step 2
: Splitting the event
E_{\ell}
.

We treat
a>0
as a constant that will be determined at the very end of the proof (the constants
c,C
appearing in the estimates will not depend on
a
). For convenience, set for the decreasing Markov chain
M
,

\Delta M_{t}\coloneqq M_{t-1}-M_{t},\qquad t\geq 1.

Note that
\Delta M_{t}\geq 1
for all
t\geq 1
.
Let
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
and
k\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
. We first partition
E_{\ell}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(E_{\ell}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\exists b^{\prime}\leq b:\max\{\Delta M_{1},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\}\leq\ell,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}3\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{b^{\prime}=0}^{b}\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\{\Delta M_{1},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\}\leq\ell,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}3\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell}}\right).

(6.34)

Note that even if the event
\{k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell\}
is contained in
\{\max\{\Delta M_{1},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\}\leq\ell\}
, it will be convenient for us to keep both events explicit.
Fix
b^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},b\mathclose{\rrbracket}
. Using the Markov property,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\{\Delta M_{1},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\}\leq\ell,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}3\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathds{1}\{\max\{\Delta M_{1},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\}\leq\ell\}\mathds{1}\{k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell\}\mathbb{P}_{M_{b^{\prime}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Delta M_{1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}3\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)}}\right].

Recalling that
k\geq 6\ell
, on the event
\{k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell\}
, we have
M_{b^{\prime}}\geq 5\ell
. Moreover,
M_{b^{\prime}}\leq k\leq 7\ell
. Therefore, we can use
Item

2
in
6.12
(with
c_{1}=1
,
c_{2}=4
,
j\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
,
x=j-4\ell
and
y=j-3\ell
)
to bound, on the event
\{k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell\}
, the probability
\mathbb{P}_{M_{b^{\prime}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Delta M_{1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}3\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)
from below:

\mathbb{P}_{M_{b^{\prime}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Delta M_{1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}3\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)=\mathbb{P}_{M_{b^{\prime}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}M_{b^{\prime}}-4\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},M_{b^{\prime}}-3\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\geq c\frac{\ell}{M_{b^{\prime}}}Q(\ell)\geq c\,Q(\ell).

This yields

\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\{\Delta M_{1},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\}\leq\ell,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}+1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}3\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell}}\right)\\
\geq c\,Q(\ell)\cdot\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\{\Delta M_{1},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\}\leq\ell,k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell}}\right).

(6.35)

For future purposes, we note that, assuming
M_{0}=k
, we can rewrite the event
\{k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell\}
as

\{k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell\}=\bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}\Delta M_{i}\mathds{1}_{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell}\leq\ell\bigg\}.

Likewise, we will need to introduce a cutoff in order to later apply the estimates of
6.12
.
Therefore, we rewrite the event

\displaystyle\{\max\{\Delta M_{1},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\}\leq\ell,k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell\}

\displaystyle=\{\max\{\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{M_{0}\geq k-\ell},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq k-\ell}\}\leq\ell,\ \sum_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}\Delta M_{i}\mathds{1}_{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell}\leq\ell\}

\displaystyle=\{\max\{\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{M_{0}\geq k-\ell},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq k-\ell}\}\leq\ell,\ \sum_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}\Delta M_{i}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{i}\leq\ell}\mathds{1}_{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell}\leq\ell\}.

Hence, the probability on the right-hand side of (
6.35
) is

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\{\Delta M_{1},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\}\leq\ell,k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\{\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{M_{0}\geq k-\ell},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq k-\ell}\}\leq\ell,\ \sum_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}\Delta M_{i}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{i}\leq\ell}\mathds{1}_{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell}\leq\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\{\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{M_{0}\geq k-\ell},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq k-\ell}\}\leq\ell}}\right)-\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}\Delta M_{i}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{i}\leq\ell}\mathds{1}_{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell}>\ell}}\right).

(6.36)

Step 3
: Lower bound for the first term in (
6.3.4
).
We deal with the first term in (
6.3.4
), bringing it down by successive applications of the Markov property to

\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\{\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{M_{0}\geq k-\ell},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq k-\ell}\}\leq\ell}}\right)\geq(1-CQ(\ell))^{b^{\prime}}

(6.37)

for some constant
C>0
.
Indeed, by the Markov property,

\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\{\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{M_{0}\geq k-\ell},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq k-\ell}\}\leq\ell}}\right)\\
=\mathbb{E}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathds{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\max\{\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{M_{0}\geq k-\ell},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}-1}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-2}\geq k-\ell}\}\leq\ell}}\right\}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq k-\ell}\leq\ell\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{b^{\prime}-1}}}\right)}}\right].

(6.38)

Now fix
j\leq 7\ell
. One can write

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq k-\ell}\leq\ell\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{b^{\prime}-1}=j}}\right)=1-\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq k-\ell}>\ell\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{b^{\prime}-1}=j}}\right).

Since
k\geq 6\ell
, the last term is smaller than
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq 5\ell}>\ell\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{b^{\prime}-1}=j}}\right)
. Besides, since
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
, we can use
Item

3
in
6.12
(with
c_{1}=6/7
,
c_{2}=6
and
y=j-\ell-1
) to see that, for all
j\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
,

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq 5\ell}>\ell\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{b^{\prime}-1}=j}}\right)=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M_{1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},j-\ell-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{0}=j}}\right)\leq CQ(\ell).

The last bound actually holds for all
j\leq 7\ell
, since the probability on the left is zero if
j<5\ell
, so we proved that

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{M_{b^{\prime}-1}\geq k-\ell}\leq\ell\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M_{b^{\prime}-1}}}\right)\geq 1-CQ(\ell).

Plugging this back into (
6.38
), we obtain (
6.37
) by induction on
b^{\prime}
.

Step 4
: Upper bound for the second term in (
6.3.4
).
We now deal with the second term of (
6.3.4
). By Markov’s inequality,

\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}\Delta M_{i}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{i}\leq\ell}\mathds{1}_{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell}>\ell}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Delta M_{i}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{i}\leq\ell}\mathds{1}_{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell}}}\right].

By the Markov property, each expectation is

\mathbb{E}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\Delta M_{i}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{i}\leq\ell}\mathds{1}_{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell}}}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathds{1}_{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell}\mathbb{E}_{M_{i-1}}\big[\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{1}\leq\ell}\big]}}\right].

But now, on the event
\{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell\}
, we have
7\ell\geq k\geq M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell\geq 5\ell
,
i.e.

M_{i-1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
. Hence, the inner expectation can be bounded using
Item

4
in
6.12
as

\mathbb{E}_{M_{i-1}}\big[\Delta M_{1}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{1}\leq\ell}\big]\leq C\ell Q(\ell).

We deduce

\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{b^{\prime}}\Delta M_{i}\mathds{1}_{\Delta M_{i}\leq\ell}\mathds{1}_{M_{i-1}\geq k-\ell}>\ell}}\right)\leq Cb^{\prime}Q(\ell).

Step 5
: Completing the proof of (
6.33
).

Going back to (
6.3.4
), the bounds from Steps 3 and 4 give

\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\max\{\Delta M_{1},\dots,\Delta M_{b^{\prime}}\}\leq\ell,k-M_{b^{\prime}}\leq\ell}}\right)\geq(1-CQ(\ell))^{b^{\prime}}-Cb^{\prime}Q(\ell).

Then (
6.34
)–(
6.35
) provide, for some constants
c,C>0
,

\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(E_{\ell}}}\right)\geq c\cdot Q(\ell)\cdot\bigg(\frac{1-(1-CQ(\ell))^{b+1}}{CQ(\ell)}-Cb^{2}Q(\ell)\bigg)\geq\frac{c}{C}\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-(1-CQ(\ell))^{b}-C^{2}(bQ(\ell))^{2}}}\right).

We recall that
b=b(\ell)\coloneqq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\lceil\frac{a}{Q(\ell)}}}\right\rceil
.
To conclude, it remains to see that one can choose
a>0
(small enough) so that the right-hand side is bounded from below by a positive constant, uniformly over
\ell
.
For the first term, we note that

1-(1-CQ(\ell))^{b}\geq 1-\mathrm{e}^{-CbQ(\ell)}\geq 1-\mathrm{e}^{-Ca}.

On the other hand, we can bound the second term by
C^{2}(a+Q(\ell))^{2}\leq 2C^{2}a^{2}
for
\ell
large enough (where the threshold only depends on
a
). Overall we have proven the existence of two constants
c>0
and
C>0
such that for all
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
large enough and
k\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
,

\mathbb{P}_{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(E_{\ell}}}\right)\geq c(1-\mathrm{e}^{-Ca}-2C^{2}a^{2}).

We can now take
a
small enough so that this is positive, and remove the condition that
\ell
is large enough by using
\mathbb{P}_{k}(E_{\ell})>0
for all
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
and
k\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
. This concludes the proof of (
6.33
).
∎

Proof of
Item

3
of
6.14
.

Let
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
.
Recalling that
\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}=m^{\prime}}}\right)=\pi(m;m^{\prime})
and using
Item

1
in
6.12
, we get that for all
m\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
,

\forall m^{\prime}\leq 4\ell,\quad\frac{c}{m}\cdot m^{\prime}\cdot q(m^{\prime})\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}=m^{\prime}}}\right)\leq\frac{C}{m}\cdot m^{\prime}\cdot q(m^{\prime}),

and so

\frac{c}{m}\sum_{m^{\prime}=\ell}^{4\ell}m^{\prime}\cdot q(m^{\prime})\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\leq\frac{C}{m}\sum_{m^{\prime}=\ell}^{4\ell}m^{\prime}\cdot q(m^{\prime}).

As a consequence, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\forall m\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\quad\forall m^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\quad\frac{1}{C}\cdot\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{5\ell}=m^{\prime}}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}=m^{\prime}}}\right)\leq C\cdot\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{5\ell}=m^{\prime}}}\right),

(6.39)

and

\displaystyle\forall m\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\quad\frac{1}{C}\cdot\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{5\ell}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\leq C\cdot\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{5\ell}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right).

(6.40)

Combining (
6.39
) and (
6.40
), we get that for all
m\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
,

\displaystyle\forall m^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\quad\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{m}=m^{\prime}}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:red-law}}}{{=}}\frac{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}=m^{\prime}}}\right)}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)}\geq\frac{1}{C^{2}}\cdot\frac{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{5\ell}=m^{\prime}}}\right)}{\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{5\ell}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)}=\frac{1}{C^{2}}\cdot\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{5\ell}=m^{\prime}}}\right).

This ensures that

\sum_{m^{\prime}=\ell}^{4\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\inf_{m\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{m}=m^{\prime}}}\right)}}\right)\geq c\sum_{m^{\prime}=\ell}^{4\ell}\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{5\ell}=m^{\prime}}}\right)=c,

(6.41)

where in the last equality we used that the sum is one by definition (
6.17
) of
G_{5\ell}
. By standard arguments (see for example
[
Pre22
]
), this allows us to construct a coupling
G_{m}
,
m\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
, of the golden instructions such that
17
17

17

Even if not strictly needed, we note that here we can take the same constant
c
as in (
6.41
).

\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\forall a,a^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\ G_{a}=G_{a^{\prime}}}}\right)\geq c.

This proves our claim.
∎

As explained in
Section

6.3.2
, we also need to show that the facts stated in
Item

2
of
6.14
for
M
also hold for
M^{\prime}
, conditional on them holding for
M
, with uniformly positive probability across all good scales.

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
M
and
M^{\prime}
be defined as the decreasing chains started from respectively
k
and
k^{\prime}
under the black-golden coupling
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}
of
6.5
.
Let
c\in(0,1)
be the constant in
6.14
. Then, for any
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
and for all
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
, we have that

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{4\ell}-1)\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{4\ell})\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}M(H_{4\ell}-1)\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},M(H_{4\ell})\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\geq\frac{c}{2}.

Proof.

Fix
\ell\in\mathrm{GoodScales}
and
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}
, and define the finite set of paths

\mathcal{M}_{k,\ell}\coloneqq\Big\{\vec{m}=(m_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},h-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}:h>0,\,\,k=m_{0}>m_{1}>\ldots>m_{h-1},\,\,m_{h-1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\Big\}.

We then have

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{M_{0}=k,M(H_{4\ell}-1)\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},M(H_{4\ell})\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket})}}\right\}\\
=\bigsqcup_{\vec{m}\in\mathcal{M}_{k,\ell}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right\},

where we recall that
B_{m_{h-1}}
is the black instruction at height
m_{h-1}
.
We now fix a path
\vec{m}=(m_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},h-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}\in\mathcal{M}_{k,\ell}
and set
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}\coloneqq\{M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{4\ell}-1)\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{4\ell})\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\}
. We will show that

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\geq\frac{c}{2}.

(6.42)

Note that this immediately implies the lemma statement.
We need to introduce some new notation. Define, for all
t^{\prime}>0
, the finite set of paths

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}(\vec{m},t^{\prime})\coloneqq\Big\{\vec{m}^{\prime}=(m^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},t^{\prime}\mathclose{\rrbracket}}:k^{\prime}=m^{\prime}_{0}>m^{\prime}_{1}>\ldots>m^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}},\\
m^{\prime}_{t^{\prime}}=m_{t}\text{ for some $t\leq h-1$},\,\,m^{\prime}_{i}\neq m_{j}\text{ for all $i<t^{\prime}$ and $j<t$}\Big\},

that is, the set of all possible paths for
M^{\prime}
up to
t^{\prime}
that are merging into the path
\vec{m}
at time
t^{\prime}
. We also define the set

{\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime}}}(\vec{m})\coloneqq\Big\{\vec{m}^{\prime}=(m^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},h^{\prime}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}:h^{\prime}>0,\,\,k^{\prime}=m^{\prime}_{0}>m^{\prime}_{1}>\ldots>m^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}-1},\\
m^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}-1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket},\,\,m^{\prime}_{i}\neq m_{j}\text{ for all $i\leq h^{\prime}-1$ and $j\leq h-1$}\Big\},

that is, the set of all possible paths for
M^{\prime}
up to
H^{\prime}_{4\ell}-1
on the event
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}
that are not merging into the path
\vec{m}
up to time
h-1
.
We also introduce the events

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigsqcup_{t^{\prime}>0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(M^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},t^{\prime}\mathclose{\rrbracket}}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}(\vec{m},t^{\prime})}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(M^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H^{\prime}_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime}}(\vec{m})\,,\,B_{M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{4\ell}-1)}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right\}.

Note that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}
, and on the event
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right\}
,

\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}=\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}\sqcup\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}.

(6.43)

We divide our analysis of (
6.42
) into two cases.

Case 1
:
We first assume that
\mathbb{P}_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}})\geq\frac{c}{2}
. Note that thanks to (
6.43
),

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}}\right)\\
=\sum_{t^{\prime}>0}\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((M^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},t^{\prime}\mathclose{\rrbracket}}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}(\vec{m},t^{\prime})\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}}\right).

(6.44)

Now, fix
t^{\prime}>0
. For any path
\vec{m}^{\prime}=(m^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},t^{\prime}\mathclose{\rrbracket}}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}(\vec{m},t^{\prime})
, the event
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(M^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},t^{\prime}\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m}^{\prime}}}\right\}
is measurable w.r.t. the black instructions
(B_{m^{\prime}_{i}})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},t^{\prime}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}
, while
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right\}
is measurable w.r.t. the black instructions
(B_{m_{i}})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},h-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}
.
But, by the definition of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}(\vec{m},t^{\prime})
, we have that
m^{\prime}_{i}\neq m_{j}
for all
i<t^{\prime}
and
j<t
(and so also for all
j\leq h-1
since both paths are decreasing and
m_{t^{\prime}}^{\prime}=m_{t}
).
Therefore, these two events are independent by the independence of the black instructions. As a result, we have

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)=\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}}}\right)\geq\frac{c}{2}

(6.45)

by our assumption that
\mathbb{P}_{k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}}}\right)\geq\frac{c}{2}
.
Combining (
6.44
) and (
6.45
), we get (
6.42
).

Case 2
:
We now assume that
\mathbb{P}_{k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}}}\right)<\frac{c}{2}
.
Because of
Equation

6.43
, we have the bound

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\\
\geq\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right).

(6.46)

Now, for any
\vec{m}^{\prime}=(m^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},h^{\prime}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}\in\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime}}(\vec{m})
, the event
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(M^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H^{\prime}_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m}^{\prime},B_{m^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right\}
is measurable w.r.t. the black instructions
(B_{m^{\prime}_{i}})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},h^{\prime}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}
,
while
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right\}
is measurable w.r.t. the black instructions
(B_{m_{i}})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},h-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}
.
But, by definition of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\,\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime}}(\vec{m})
, we have that
m^{\prime}_{i}\neq m_{j}
for all
i\leq h^{\prime}-1
and
j\leq h
and thus these two events are independent.
Combined with (
6.46
), this shows that

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}(M_{i})_{i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}0\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},H_{4\ell}-1\mathclose{\rrbracket}}=\vec{m},B_{m_{h-1}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right)\geq\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}}}\right).

(6.47)

Now, since
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}\subset\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{c}}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}\cup\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}
, we can write

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}}}\right)=\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}^{\mathrm{c}}}}\right)+\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}}}\right).

Thanks to
Item

2
in
6.14
, we have that
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\ell}}}\right)\geq c
. Since
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}})<\frac{c}{2}
by assumption, we immediately get that

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{k^{\prime},\vec{m}}^{\mathrm{c}}}}\right)>\frac{c}{2}.

(6.48)

Thus, we get (
6.42
) as a consequence of (
6.47
) and (
6.48
).

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.3.5
Completing the coupling argument: fast merging of the two walks

We are now ready to complete the proof of
6.6
.

Proof of
6.6
.

Most of the proof will be devoted to establishing
Item

1
.
The gist of the proof is the following: by Items
1
and
2
of
6.14
(combined with
6.15
), at each good scale, there is positive probability that both
M
and
M^{\prime}
follow a golden instruction, and therefore positive probability to merge thanks to
Item

3
of
6.14
. Moreover, by the specific conditioning in
Item

1
of
6.14
, we can iterate this argument at each good scale, so this will happen at a fair amount of scales. Since there are infinitely many good scales, it will happen with probability going to
1
as the starting points
k
and
k^{\prime}
of
M
and
M^{\prime}
go to infinity.

We now make this argument rigorous.
Without loss of generality, we assume that
k^{\prime}\geq k
.
Fix
A\geq 1
. For
k\geq A
, we recall that
s=s(A,k)
is the number of good scales
\ell
in
\mathopen{\llbracket}A\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},k\mathclose{\rrbracket}
such that
7\ell<k
.
We denote, for all
k\geq 1
,

\ell^{1}=\ell^{1}(k)\coloneqq\max\{\ell^{\prime}\in\mathrm{GoodScales}\,:\,7\ell^{\prime}<k\},

(6.49)

with the convention
\ell^{1}(k)=0
if no such scale exists, and then write
\ell^{i}=\ell^{i}(k)
for the
i
-th good scale down from
\ell^{1}(k)
.

We start by noting that
M
and
M^{\prime}
will meet above height
A
if all the following events occur simultaneously at some scale
\ell^{i}
with
1\leq i\leq s
(recall
Figure

11
):

•

The chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
, at times
H_{7\ell^{i}}
and
H^{\prime}_{7\ell^{i}}
respectively, land in the sub-interval
\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell^{i}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell^{i}\mathclose{\rrbracket}
(highlighted in blue in
Figure

11
).

•

The chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
both exit the interval
\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell^{i}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell^{i}\mathclose{\rrbracket}
by following a golden instruction,
i.e.
landing in the golden sub-interval in
Figure

11
.
This means that
M\big(H_{4\ell^{i}}-1\big)\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell^{i}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell^{i}\mathclose{\rrbracket}
and the black instruction
B_{M(H_{4\ell^{i}}-1)}
is inside the interval
\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell^{i}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell^{i}\mathclose{\rrbracket}
, and that the same holds for
M^{\prime}
.

•

The golden instructions
G_{M(H_{4\ell^{i}}-1)}
and
G_{M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{4\ell^{i}}-1)}
are equal.

Therefore, for any
i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},s\mathclose{\rrbracket}
we introduce the event

\mathcal{E}_{i}\coloneqq\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}^{3},

(6.50)

where

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}&=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{M(H_{7\ell^{i}})\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell^{i}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell^{i}\mathclose{\rrbracket}}}\right\},\\
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}&=\Big\{M\big(H_{4\ell^{i}}-1\big)\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell^{i}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell^{i}\mathclose{\rrbracket},B_{M(H_{4\ell^{i}}-1)}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}\ell^{i}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},4\ell^{i}\mathclose{\rrbracket}\Big\},\\
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{3}&=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\forall a,a^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}5\ell^{i}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell^{i}\mathclose{\rrbracket},\ G_{a}=G_{a^{\prime}}}}\right\},\end{aligned}}}\right.

and
(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}
and
(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}
are constructed similarly to
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}
but for the chain
M^{\prime}
.
Recall the
\sigma
-field
\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}
of (
6.29
).
Note that for any
j<i
, the event
\mathcal{E}_{j}
is
\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}
-measurable.

The preceding discussion leads to

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{\mer}<A}}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^{s}\mathcal{E}_{i}^{\mathrm{c}}}}\right).

(6.51)

We start by proving that for any
i\in\mathopen{\llbracket}1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},s\mathclose{\rrbracket}
we almost surely have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right)\geq c^{5}/2,

(6.52)

where
c\in(0,1)
is as in
6.14
.
We consider the events sequentially.

Bound on
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right)
:
By
Item

1
of
6.13
together with
Item

1
of
6.14
,
we get that on the event
\{M(H_{7\ell^{i}}-1)=m,M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell^{i}}-1)=m^{\prime}\}
with

m\in\mathopen{\llbracket}7\ell^{i}+1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},k\mathclose{\rrbracket}
and
m^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}7\ell^{i}+1\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},k^{\prime}\mathclose{\rrbracket}
such that
m\neq m^{\prime}
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m},B_{m^{\prime}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}B_{m}\leq 7\ell,B_{m^{\prime}}\leq 7\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}B_{m}\leq 7\ell}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(B_{m^{\prime}}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell\mathclose{\rrbracket}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}B_{m^{\prime}}\leq 7\ell}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq c^{2}.

Moreover, if
m=m^{\prime}
, the same computation gives a lower bound
c\geq c^{2}
, so the last inequality is true for every possible value
(m,m^{\prime})
that can be taken by the pair
(M(H_{7\ell^{i}}-1),M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell^{i}}-1))
. Therefore, we almost surely have

\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right)\geq c^{2}.

(6.53)

Bound on
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right)

:
Let
j,j^{\prime}\in\mathopen{\llbracket}6\ell^{i}\mathclose{}\mathpunct{},7\ell^{i}\mathclose{\rrbracket}
.
Conditioning on
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}
, we have

\mathbb{P}_{j,j^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}}}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{j}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{j,j^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}}}\right)}}\right].

From there, we use
6.15
to get
\mathbb{P}_{j,j^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}}}\right)\geq c/2
and then Item
2
in
6.14
to obtain

\mathbb{P}_{j,j^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}}}\right)\geq c^{2}/2.

(6.54)

Note that the events
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}
and
(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}
are realized for the chains
M
and
M^{\prime}
if and only if they are realized for the chains
(M(H_{7\ell^{i}}+t))_{t\geq 0}
and
(M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell^{i}}+t))_{t\geq 0}
.
By
Item

2
of
6.13
, this ensures that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}_{M(H_{7\ell^{i}}),M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{7\ell^{i}})}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}}}\right)\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathclap{\eqref{eq:bnd_evnt2_j}}}{\geq}\,\,\frac{c^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right]\overset{\eqref{eq:bnd_evnt1_i}}{\geq}\frac{c^{4}}{2}

almost surely.

Bound on
\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right)
:
Finally, by independence of black and golden instructions, and independence of golden instructions between different scales, the event
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{3}
is independent of the chains up to the times
H_{4\ell^{i}}-1
and
H^{\prime}_{4\ell^{i}}-1
and of
B_{M(H_{4\ell^{i}}-1)}
and
B_{M^{\prime}(H^{\prime}_{4\ell^{i}}-1)}
, so it is independent of
\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}
.
Since it is also independent of
\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}
, we have, using Item
3
of
6.14
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}^{3}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right)=\mathbb{P}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{3}}}\right)\mathbb{P}_{k,k^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{1})^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2}\cap(\mathcal{E}_{i}^{2})^{\prime}\mathrel{}\middle|\mathrel{}\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i}}}}\right)\geq c\cdot\frac{c^{4}}{2}\geq\frac{c^{5}}{2}

almost surely, which proves (
6.52
).

Now, using in cascade that
\mathcal{E}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{E}_{i}
are
\mathcal{G}_{\ell^{i+1}}
-measurable together with the fact (
6.52
) we get
